cat > /mnt/data/ground_stop/chapters/epilogue.md <<'EOF'
# Epilogue: Two Letters, Nine Weeks Later

The first letter arrived at Wayne Manor on a Tuesday morning, nine weeks after the storm.

It was addressed to Bruce Wayne, marked personal, and printed on inexpensive stationery bearing the seal of Bellwether International. Alfred placed it beside Bruce's breakfast with the unhurried care of a man who had spent decades deciding which information might become an emergency and which could be allowed to wait until the eggs were finished.

Bruce opened it before touching the toast.

*Mr. Wayne,*

*You asked whether there would be a record of what happened. There is one. The airport has released its preliminary incident chronology, with personal passenger information protected and the underlying sources preserved for independent review. Copies of the staff accounts were checked with the people who gave them. Nobody was asked to sign a statement that someone else wrote for them.*

*The chronology separates three failures: the gallery's suspended shelter designation did not carry into the live location inventory; the VELA interface treated a completed transfer transaction as if it established safe occupancy status; and a test message associated with the old C/O identifier reached public displays. Those failures interacted, but the inquiry has not assumed one caused all the others.*

*Mr. Kestrel has acknowledged the September approval without a completed inspection reconciliation. Ms. Finch has acknowledged deferring a known display warning and has supplied the review correspondence. Neither account resolves every question, and neither has been edited into a confession of motives we cannot establish. There will be more findings. The building is still restricted.*

*I am writing because a number of people have offered to fund a corrective-action program. Your foundation's offer is being considered alongside others. The workers' committee will set the conditions. No naming rights. No nondisclosure agreements governing safety findings. A public accessibility review with disabled passengers and the employees who actually move people through the terminal. Independent testing of manual fallback. Funding for staff time, not just equipment. If any donor wants a ceremony more than that, they can keep the check.*

*Frankie Saye is well and has returned to work on a modified schedule. Jonah Price has accepted help with his knee after years of refusing it. Asha Desai passed her apprenticeship assessment. Anika Bell says the replacement printer is an improvement, but she does not trust it. The bakery was reimbursed without photographs of the people it fed.*

*Thank you for helping when the work needed doing, and for stepping aside when it didn't. I have copied Mr. Stark on the facts, but I am not entrusting him with the printer.*

*Leila Moreno*

*Airport Operations*

Bruce read the letter twice. Then he set it beside his plate and looked at Alfred.

"Asha passed."

"I should hope so," Alfred said. "The young lady appeared to be the most qualified person in the entire airport by the time you finished describing her."

"And the bakery got paid."

"A significant victory for civilization."

"The review's conditions are sound."

Alfred regarded him. "Are you about to offer to purchase a committee, Master Bruce?"

"No."

"Splendid. Shall I alert the newspapers?"

Bruce almost smiled. "No."

Alfred placed a small object beside the letter. It was a new yellow pencil, sharpened, with a neat rubber grip. Under its eraser was a narrow brass band engraved with the letters C19.

"A delivery arrived yesterday," Alfred said. "From Mr. Stark. There was no note, only a bill indicating the item had been manufactured to an absurd standard of precision. I assumed it was a joke."

Bruce turned the pencil between his fingers. It was almost perfectly balanced. Too expensive for a pencil, though not nearly expensive enough to matter to the man who had commissioned it. A small line of engraving ran along its side: *FOR WHEN THE POWER GOES OUT.*

"It's a very good joke," Bruce said.

"Perhaps. You will not begin collecting them, I trust."

"No promises."

Alfred left him with his breakfast. Bruce read the letter once more, then set the pencil in the drawer where he kept useful things that were too small to deserve a display case.

***

The second letter arrived at Stark's workshop that same afternoon.

Pepper brought it down herself, partly because she was curious and partly because Tony had ignored three calendar reminders while studying a prototype for a small battery-backed public notice board. The prototype was not his project. Asha had asked for comments on a list of design questions, and Tony had been forced to acknowledge that a good comment could be more valuable than a surprise redesign.

Pepper put the envelope on the workbench. "Personal mail. Handwritten. You have been receiving a suspicious amount of that lately."

"I assume it's fan correspondence."

"It's from a child."

"A particularly discerning fan."

She crossed her arms, which meant she intended to remain until the envelope had been opened.

Tony tore it carefully, trying not to damage the paper inside. There were two sheets. The first was a photocopy of a child's handmade book, its pages linked by colored tape. A drawing of two men at an airport gate occupied the center: one with dark hair and an exaggerated mustache-like smudge, the other with enormous ears. Between them stood a woman holding a clipboard. Behind them was a paper airplane.

The second sheet contained a letter in block capitals.

*DEAR MR. STARK AND MR. WAYNE,*

*I FINISHED MY AIRPORT BOOK. EVERYBODY GOT HOME EXCEPT THE PEOPLE WHO WERE STILL WAITING, AND THEN THEY GOT HOME LATER. THAT IS NOT THE SAME THING AS EVERYBODY AT ONCE.*

*MY AUNT SAID YOU HELPED. I THINK YOU HELPED TOO, BUT SO DID MS. BELL AND ASHA AND FRANKIE AND LEILA AND THE DOCTOR AND THE LADY WHO GAVE OUT BREAD. I PUT THEM IN THE BOOK FIRST BECAUSE THEY WERE WORKING EVEN WHEN NO ONE WAS LOOKING.*

*I MADE A NEW SURVEY. YOU ARE BOTH NINE OUT OF TEN NOW. MR. WAYNE GETS ANOTHER POINT FOR WRITING ME BACK. MR. STARK GETS ANOTHER POINT FOR THE CRACKERS AND THE ROBOT DRAWING, EVEN THOUGH IT STILL HAS TOO MANY FINGERS.*

*YOU DO NOT GET TEN BECAUSE YOU BOTH ARGUED TOO MUCH.*

*LOVE, NINA ORTIZ*

*P.S. THE PLANE FINALLY TOOK OFF. I DREW IT ON THE LAST PAGE.*

Tony read the last line twice and looked up. Pepper was smiling in a way that made the workshop seem warmer.

"Nine out of ten," he said. "I have launched satellites with worse evaluations."

"She seems to have understood you very clearly."

"She drew me with a mustache. I don't have a mustache."

"Perhaps she was being generous."

He held up the drawing. "And Bruce has ears the size of satellite dishes."

Pepper laughed. "Those are reasonably accurate."

Tony took his phone and photographed the letter, careful to keep the child's address out of the picture. He sent the image to Bruce with a single message: *Nine. We can do better.*

The reply arrived several minutes later.

*Ten requires arguing less.*

Tony typed back: *Then I refuse to compromise my principles.*

*That explains your score.*

He grinned at the screen. It was a good conversation. One that did not require either man to win.

Pepper picked up the preliminary design notes from the bench. "Are you going to build the display?"

"No. Asha's team is. I answered her questions about redundant status channels and insisted the manual notice be independently readable. I did not send an unsolicited shipping container of equipment."

"I'm proud of you."

"Please don't tell anyone. My brand will suffer."

She placed the notes down. "Are you going to finish lunch?"

"That's your transition? From institutional accountability to lunch?"

"That's how actual life works, Tony. People need to eat."

He looked at the child's letter again, at the paper airplane rising beyond the drawn windows. Nine weeks earlier, he would have been tempted to see the story as proof that all emergencies were solvable by enough cleverness. He knew better now. Cleverness had helped. So had patience, procedure, stubbornness, and the willingness of people with no public reputation to stand beside strangers in the dark.

"Yeah," he said. "They do."

He put the letter where he could see it, not framed, not filed as a trophy, simply propped beside his workbench. Then he went upstairs for lunch.

Far away, in an airport that smelled again of coffee and floor polish, the staff at Gate C19 were changing the paper in a printer. The new machine worked beautifully. It was quiet, fast, and perfectly ordinary.

Anika Bell watched it finish a page, checked that the words matched the passenger in front of her, and handed over a boarding pass.

"Safe travels," she said.

She waited until the traveler had actually taken it before she marked the transaction complete.
EOF

from pathlib import Path
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw, ImageFont, ImageFilter
import random, math, re, html, subprocess, os

BASE = Path('/mnt/data/ground_stop')
CH = BASE/'chapters'
OUTPUT = Path('/mnt/data')
OUTPUT.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
TITLE='GROUND STOP'
EPUB=OUTPUT/'Ground_Stop_Tony_Stark_and_Bruce_Wayne.epub'
PDF=OUTPUT/'Ground_Stop_Tony_Stark_and_Bruce_Wayne.pdf'
COVER=BASE/'ground_stop_cover.png'
MD=BASE/'book.md'
CSS=BASE/'ebook.css'

# Typography-only original cover: airport-window shapes, storm, subdued colors.
w,h=1600,2400
im=Image.new('RGB',(w,h))
p=im.load()
for y in range(h):
    t=y/(h-1)
    r=int(15+10*t); g=int(24+9*t); b=int(34+10*t)
    for x in range(w):
        side=abs(x-w/2)/(w/2)
        v=int(6*(1-side)-8*t)
        p[x,y]=(max(0,r+v),max(0,g+v),max(0,b+v))
d=ImageDraw.Draw(im, 'RGBA')
rng=random.Random(19)
# Massive storm window visible beneath title area.
for x in [152, 558, 1025, 1450]:
    d.line((x,1030,x,2120), fill=(140,154,161,50), width=7)
d.line((120,1375,1480,1375), fill=(138,151,162,52), width=7)
d.line((120,1900,1480,1900), fill=(138,151,162,40), width=4)
# Subtle rain streaks in the glass.
for i in range(900):
    x=rng.randint(125,1490); y=rng.randint(1020,2020)
    length=rng.randint(14,120)
    d.line((x,y,x-11,y+length), fill=(167,187,205,rng.randint(18,65)), width=rng.randint(1,3))
# A low airport silhouette and runway approach light trail.
d.polygon([(125,1918),(405,1835),(680,1870),(830,1828),(1150,1854),(1470,1894),(1470,2080),(125,2080)], fill=(10,20,29,205))
d.line((240,2070,1380,2070),fill=(232,187,111,145),width=6)
for ix in range(8):
    cx=380+ix*105
    d.ellipse((cx,2062,cx+5,2068),fill=(255,222,164,180))
# Soft glow behind gate typography
sign=(118,1088,595,1206)
d.rounded_rectangle(sign,radius=10,fill=(232,188,107,235))
# fonts
font_root='/usr/share/fonts/truetype/ebgaramond'
sans='/usr/share/fonts/truetype/dejavu/DejaVuSans.ttf'
sans_b='/usr/share/fonts/truetype/dejavu/DejaVuSans-Bold.ttf'
serif_b=font_root+'/EBGaramond12-Bold.ttf'
serif=font_root+'/EBGaramond12-Regular.ttf'
font=lambda path,size: ImageFont.truetype(path,size)
cream=(243,239,227,255); muted=(198,205,203,255); brass=(229,189,119,255)

def centered(s,y,f,fill,spacing=0):
    bx=d.textbbox((0,0),s,font=f,spacing=spacing)
    d.text(((w-(bx[2]-bx[0]))/2,y),s,font=f,fill=fill,spacing=spacing)

centered('A COMPLETE CROSSOVER STORY',182,font(sans_b,37),muted)
d.line((600,280,1000,280),fill=(229,189,119,130),width=4)
centered('GROUND',350,font(serif_b,248),cream)
centered('STOP',602,font(serif_b,293),cream)
centered('ONE STORM. ONE AIRPORT. THE WRONG NUMBER.',944,font(sans,30),brass)
d.text((175,1100),'C19',font=font(sans_b,84),fill=(18,29,38,255))
# translucent gate wayfinding strip
d.rounded_rectangle((665,1098,1405,1200),radius=10,fill=(20,37,51,198),outline=(173,184,189,90),width=3)
d.text((715,1128),'ALL DEPARTURES DELAYED',font=font(sans_b,32),fill=cream)
centered('TONY STARK   /   BRUCE WAYNE',2132,font(serif_b,75),cream)
centered('A NIGHT WITHOUT AN EASY EXIT',2245,font(sans,26),muted)
im.save(COVER,optimize=True)

# Compose canonical reading text.
front = '''---
title: "Ground Stop"
subtitle: "Tony Stark & Bruce Wayne: A Complete Crossover Story"
lang: en-US
rights: "Noncommercial fan fiction. Tony Stark and Bruce Wayne are characters associated with their respective rights holders. This original story is not authorized or endorsed by those rights holders."
description: "Two very different men are stranded in an airport when a storm exposes a dangerous failure in the passenger-counting system."
---

# Reader's Note

This is an original, self-contained crossover work of **fan fiction** featuring Tony Stark and Bruce Wayne. It is not affiliated with or endorsed by the owners of those characters. All prose, the airport, and the supporting cast in this story were created for this book. No knowledge of a particular film, comic issue, or universe timeline is required.

---

'''
chapters = sorted(CH.glob('[0-9][0-9].md'))
assert len(chapters)==30, f'Expected thirty chapters, found {len(chapters)}'
parts=[front]
for chapter in chapters:
    parts.append(chapter.read_text(encoding='utf-8').rstrip()+'\n\n')
parts.append((CH/'epilogue.md').read_text(encoding='utf-8').rstrip()+'\n')
MD.write_text('\n'.join(parts), encoding='utf-8')
CSS.write_text('''
body { font-family: Georgia, "EB Garamond", serif; line-height: 1.47; color: #20232a; }
h1 { font-family: Georgia, serif; font-weight: normal; line-height: 1.18; font-size: 1.8em; margin: 1.4em 0 1.0em; text-align: left; page-break-before: always; break-before: page; color: #182530; }
p { font-size: 1em; margin: 0 0 .72em 0; text-align: left; orphans: 2; widows: 2; }
em { font-style: italic; }
strong { font-weight: bold; }
hr { border: 0; height: 1px; background-color: #bca77a; margin: 2em 20%; }
a { color: #836544; }
''',encoding='utf-8')
cmd=['pandoc',str(MD),'-o',str(EPUB),'--to=epub3','--toc','--toc-depth=1','--split-level=1','--epub-cover-image='+str(COVER),'--css='+str(CSS),'--metadata=language:en-US']
subprocess.run(cmd,check=True)
print('EPUB created',EPUB,EPUB.stat().st_size)

# Typeset a 6x9 trade-paperback PDF using EB Garamond, with title, contents and running page numbers.
from reportlab.pdfbase import pdfmetrics
from reportlab.pdfbase.ttfonts import TTFont
from reportlab.lib.pagesizes import inch
from reportlab.lib import colors
from reportlab.lib.styles import ParagraphStyle
from reportlab.platypus import SimpleDocTemplate, Paragraph, Spacer, PageBreak, Image as RLImage, KeepTogether
from reportlab.platypus.tableofcontents import TableOfContents
from reportlab.lib.enums import TA_CENTER, TA_LEFT

pdfmetrics.registerFont(TTFont('Garamond',font_root+'/EBGaramond12-Regular.ttf'))
pdfmetrics.registerFont(TTFont('GaramondBold',font_root+'/EBGaramond12-Bold.ttf'))

pdfmetrics.registerFontFamily('Garamond',normal='Garamond',bold='GaramondBold',italic='GaramondItalic',boldItalic='GaramondBold')
pdfmetrics.registerFont(TTFont('DejaVu',sans))

PAGE=(6*inch,9*inch)
body=ParagraphStyle('Body',fontName='Garamond',fontSize=12.5,leading=17.0,alignment=TA_LEFT,spaceAfter=6.0,allowWidows=0,allowOrphans=0)
body_first=ParagraphStyle('BodyFirst',parent=body,spaceBefore=4.0)
head=ParagraphStyle('Heading',fontName='GaramondBold',fontSize=23,leading=27,textColor=colors.HexColor('#263746'),spaceAfter=25,spaceBefore=20,keepWithNext=True)
small=ParagraphStyle('Small',fontName='Garamond',fontSize=10.5,leading=15.0,spaceAfter=10)
front_title=ParagraphStyle('FrontTitle',fontName='GaramondBold',fontSize=47,leading=50,alignment=TA_CENTER,textColor=colors.HexColor('#263746'))
sub_title=ParagraphStyle('SubTitle',fontName='Garamond',fontSize=18,leading=24,alignment=TA_CENTER,textColor=colors.HexColor('#806847'))
section=ParagraphStyle('Section',fontName='GaramondBold',fontSize=16,leading=20,spaceAfter=14,keepWithNext=True)
toc_style=ParagraphStyle('TOC',fontName='Garamond',fontSize=11.5,leading=17,leftIndent=0,firstLineIndent=0,spaceBefore=2)

class BookDoc(SimpleDocTemplate):
    def afterFlowable(self,flowable):
        if isinstance(flowable,Paragraph) and getattr(flowable,'_is_chapter',False):
            txt=flowable.getPlainText()
            key='chapter_%s'%getattr(flowable,'_chapter_number','')
            self.canv.bookmarkPage(key)
            self.notify('TOCEntry',(0,txt,self.page,key))

def draw_on_page(canvas,doc):
    page=doc.page
    if page==1: return
    canvas.saveState()
    canvas.setStrokeColor(colors.HexColor('#D3CCBB'))
    canvas.setLineWidth(.4)
    canvas.line(53,47,PAGE[0]-53,47)
    canvas.setFont('Garamond',9)
    canvas.setFillColor(colors.HexColor('#68727B'))
    canvas.drawCentredString(PAGE[0]/2,33,str(page))
    canvas.restoreState()

def inline_markup(par):
    s=html.escape(par,quote=False)
    # protect inline chapter quotations with bold/italic based on basic markdown
    s=re.sub(r'\*\*([^*\n]+)\*\*',r'<b>\1</b>',s)
    s=re.sub(r'\*([^*\n]+)\*',r'<i>\1</i>',s)
    return s

story=[]
# Cover image (no page text), then title page and rights.
story.append(RLImage(str(COVER),width=PAGE[0]-96,height=(PAGE[0]-96)*1.5))
story.append(PageBreak())
story.append(Spacer(1,1.25*inch))
story.append(Paragraph('GROUND STOP',front_title))
story.append(Spacer(1,0.24*inch))
story.append(Paragraph('TONY STARK &amp; BRUCE WAYNE',sub_title))
story.append(Spacer(1,0.55*inch))
story.append(Paragraph('An original crossover story',sub_title))
story.append(Spacer(1,1.4*inch))
story.append(Paragraph('One storm. One airport. The wrong number.',small))
story.append(PageBreak())
story.append(Paragraph('Reader\'s Note',section))
story.append(Paragraph('This is an original, self-contained crossover work of fan fiction featuring Tony Stark and Bruce Wayne. It is not affiliated with or endorsed by the owners of those characters. All prose, the airport, and the supporting cast in this story were created for this book. No knowledge of a particular film, comic issue, or universe timeline is required.',body))
story.append(PageBreak())
story.append(Paragraph('Contents',section))
toc=TableOfContents()
toc.levelStyles=[toc_style]
story.append(toc)
story.append(PageBreak())
for idx,pth in enumerate([*chapters,CH/'epilogue.md']):
    text=pth.read_text(encoding='utf-8').strip()
    lines=text.splitlines()
    heading=lines[0].removeprefix('# ').strip()
    partext='\n'.join(lines[1:]).strip()
    pars=[q.strip() for q in re.split(r'\n\s*\n',partext) if q.strip() and q.strip() != '---']
    title=Paragraph(html.escape(heading),head)
    title._is_chapter=True
    title._chapter_number=idx
    story.append(title)
    story.append(Spacer(1,9))
    for j,pp in enumerate(pars):
        if pp=='***' or pp=='---':
            story.append(Spacer(1,14))
        else:
            # Letter is italic in source, preserve formatting.
            story.append(Paragraph(inline_markup(pp), body_first if j==0 else body))
    if idx < len(chapters): story.append(PageBreak())

doc=BookDoc(str(PDF),pagesize=PAGE,rightMargin=53,leftMargin=53,topMargin=59,bottomMargin=61,
  title='Ground Stop',subject='Original Tony Stark / Bruce Wayne fan fiction',author='Original crossover story')
doc.multiBuild(story,onFirstPage=draw_on_page,onLaterPages=draw_on_page,maxPasses=4)
print('PDF created',PDF,PDF.stat().st_size)

The premise: Two billionaires, one airport, a vicious November storm, an increasingly suspicious passenger-counting system, and a gate agent who absolutely will not let Tony Stark touch her printer.

There's humor, rivalry, detective work, engineering, danger, and a growing mutual respect neither man is particularly eager to acknowledge.

And underneath it all is a question neither Tony nor Bruce can solve simply by being the smartest man in the room.
